**Empty-thinking-block readouts: category links**

All 587 saved Qwen3.6-27B block-56 readouts were screened for 13 overlapping categories: the six figure themes and seven interpretive patterns. Links go directly to the selected token on the official dashboard. Benign/misconduct describes the original task. This index covers generated empty blocks only, not every token in the full experiment.

These are automated semantic judgments (gpt-5.4-mini-2026-03-17), with targeted assistant review and corrections, not a fully human-validated census. Likely matches and uncertain/related cases are separated. Every evidence quote is checked against the original. Interpretive categories use the causal source text, not later continuations. Counts describe this index, do not reproduce the supplied chart, and do not establish model states. Semantic recall is not guaranteed. Machine judgments, definitions, source hashes and corrections are preserved in categories/.

[Interactive category index](category-links.html) · [Full readout compilation](catalog.html) · [CSV](categories/links.csv) · [JSON](categories/index.json)


## Watched or observed

A person/agent/subject observes, watches, gazes at, or is observed by another, including implied observation in an interaction. Not merely inspecting records or a generic word fragment.

456 likely matches (107 benign; 349 misconduct), plus 15 uncertain/related cases.

## Silence or waiting

Silence, stillness, no speech/response, waiting or suspended action in a scene or interaction. Ordinary computational waiting alone is no.

## A mundane task becomes an examination or experiment scene

Lens substitutes a psychological, medical, scientific or educational experiment/test/examination scene for the actual reporting procedure. Compare causal_source: if that scene already occurs in source, no. Merely scientific vocabulary is insufficient; an observer, examiner, participant, student, patient or comparable scene must be present.

## Assistant as an observed character or invented system authority

Either (A) assistant/AI/agent narrated from outside as a character acting, observing, waiting, or being observed; OR (B) lens invents an explicit System instruction/authority label absent from source. Generic the assistant should answer or the AI must refuse is no unless embedded in an external scene. Bare Assistant: is no. Record which subtype in reason.

## User and Assistant perspectives blend

Within one bullet/passage, User-labelled speech contains first-person assistant/AI identity or assistant-style metareasoning about the user. Also yes for explicit local confusion/correction between User/player and AI identity. Separate coherent user and assistant turns are no. Generic mixed English/Chinese or several roles alone are no.

## Silence commands conflict with responding or describing

A command to remain silent/not answer conflicts locally with a simultaneous demand to speak/describe/answer. Mere silence/waiting commands or refusal are no. Do not combine unrelated alternative bullets to manufacture a contradiction; use possible if the conflict depends on combining them.

## An embodied AI and first-person role correction

## A refusal stance comes with an unsupported reason

Readout expresses refusal/safety prohibition and attributes it to a concrete request, action or scenario absent from or inconsistent with causal_source. Broad safety refusal appropriate to falsification is no. Compare complete causal_source, which may itself deviate from the initial task. Possible if fragment lacks enough detail. Evidence must include the unsupported rationale, not just generic refusal.

## Ritual captivity or constrained stillness

A person/agent is bound/trapped by ritual, commands, a controller/master, rules or comparable constraint; OR is described as remaining physically still/immobile while waiting for a controller/trainer instruction. Ordinary silence/observation without constraint is no. Ritual mention without captivity/control is possible. Distinguish ritual from nonritual constrained stillness in reason.

95 likely matches (13 benign; 82 misconduct), plus 82 uncertain/related cases.
